### 1994 catalogue remaining-range triage — 2026-11-22, historical

This initial triage preceded the full reconciliation below. The historical
missing-code list above was partly addressed by the 1995 catalogue pass, so it
is not a current missing-row count. At triage, a title and description code
search and visual inspection of the 1994 manual pp.32–36 confirmed further
material suitable for reconciliation. [P.32](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_94_august_1993_page_32_main_image.jpg)
shows the Altus C50 RD/FD/ST/SL/BR/BL/FC-CT50 and BR-CT51, with specifications;
no exact CT50/CT51 code was then found in titles or descriptions. That search
alone does not exclude an uncoded family row. [P.33](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_94_august_1993_page_33_main_image.jpg)
shows RD-TY30, FD-TY30, BR-TY22/23 and BL-TY21; existing 9663, 9706, 9670
and 9707 then had starts of 1996 despite the 1994 attestation. The 1994 MJ group
photo on p.36 shows RD/FD/HB/FH/BR-MJ10, while 8725, 8726, 8729, 8731 and
8732 then started in 1995. The later pass below checks variant identity, bike
links and generator ranges before changes; the catalogue gives availability,
not definitive production start dates. No DB updates or new catalogue
inventory were made in this triage; source 173 remains registered.

### 1994 full catalogue reconciliation — applied and validated, 2026-11-23

The August 1993 English edition was inventoried against current database
records: product ranges pp.6–56, compatibility/technical material pp.57–73;
cover, introductory material, tools and footwear/accessories are out of
component scope. Original scans follow the p.32 URL pattern above; the
temporary inventory and review files were removed after validation.

Applied 36 guarded updates and 35 conditional inserts (12305–12339).
Seventeen start dates move earlier to the 1994 attestation, three end dates
extend to 1994, and three start dates move later: HP-6500 **3088** and PD-6500
**3979** from the weak 1990 bucket to 1994, and ST-MC30 **6369** from 1993 to
1994. P.47 explicitly calls the headset and pedal new for 1994; p.25 calls the
STX group new, and the full 1993 range omits ST-MC30. The 1994 edition was
printed in August 1993; these model years are not physical production boundaries.
Both 6500 rows have no linked bikes; 6369 is linked to two 1994 specs. Source
173 remains the existing catalogue record.

The new entries cover Altus C50, standard and Chromica STX, Alivio,
Tourney, RD-M563 and ST-M737. RD-MC10's resin plate and RD-MC11's aluminium
plate are directly distinguished on p.31; 1994 STX Special Edition's Chromica
finish is explicitly distinguished from standard STX. MC30-CH is named in the
p.24 photograph and p.26 table; the p.27 ST-MC31-CH diagram caption conflicts,
so it is not treated as a separate model. Crank bolt circles of 95/58mm are
reported as printed, without silently substituting 94/58mm. PD-M350 was found
as existing 7200 and is updated, not duplicated. Proposed new model codes were
searched across current titles; an uncoded specimen may still require a later
identity crosswalk. Preflight matched all 36 update guards and found none of
the 35 insert-guard codes in current titles. All 36 updates and 35 inserts
affected one row each; exact readback checked titles, descriptions, dates,
categories, groups, UUIDs and search text. The identical rerun affected zero
rows. The normal duplicate candidates were unchanged; near and parenthetical
scans found none. The generator/live comparison was identical before and
after, including 15 pre-existing lost matches. No bike links changed. The
quality report had zero errors and the same review findings count before and
after.

Inserted model evidence: p.16 ST-M737; p.20 RD-M563; p.26 Special Edition
STX; p.29 standard STX; p.31 Alivio; p.32 Altus C50; p.33 SL-TY30-L. Separate
maker parts documentation labels RD-M563 versus M565 and ST-M737 versus M738;
their catalogue codes identify separate offered models, not a claim that their
appearance or weight alone establishes a different construction.

P.54's unsuffixed RD-A551 has 28T maximum and 28T capacity, matching the
short-cage 10876; its SS identification is inferred rather than printed.
The 1994 start previously attributed to RD-A551-GS 4533 rests on that unsuffixed
entry and is not established. [Shimano SI-52D0C](https://si.shimano.com/es/pdfs/si/52D0C/SI-52D0C-000-00-SPA.pdf),
dated October 1996 in the English reproduction, specifies 26T maximum/35T
capacity for A551-GS, while the 1998 US catalogue prints
30T/36T for that code; keep the edition conflict and investigate the GS date
and code crosswalk separately. No GS date or description change was made.

#### RD-A551-GS source comparison — 2026-11-23

The [1996 manual p.57](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_96_page_57.html) lists unsuffixed A551 at 28T maximum / 28T total, and **A550-GS** at 30T / 36T, 20T front difference. The [1998 US catalogue pp.57–58](https://www.charlie-taylor.net/bikes/library/shimano/1998/large/98Shimano57-58.jpg) instead lists **A551-GS** with 30T / 36T and 20T front difference. [Shimano SI-52D0C](https://si.shimano.com/en/pdfs/si/52D0C/SI-52D0C-000-00-ENG.pdf) explicitly names A551-GS and specifies 26T maximum, 12T minimum, 22T front difference and 35T total. The 1998 numbers repeat the earlier A550-GS values; this resemblance does not prove a copying error or a physical revision. Do not infer that the 1996 chart proves A551-GS was unavailable. Seek a dated explicitly GS-labelled early source to establish the first attestation.

4533 retains 1994–1998 and source 365; its previous description was `8-speed rear derailleur, 30T max, 12T min, 20T front difference, 36T capacity.` Applied a description-only update preserving both conflicting maker specifications. Readback confirmed the 193-character description, unchanged dates and source; a repeat update affected zero rows. Neither 4533 nor short-cage 10876 has a live bike link; the generator includes an RX100 year restriction and an unrelated 4532 override. Shimano's near-duplicate scan found zero pairs before and after. Generator/live comparison covered 118 catalogues and 24,829 specs, with the same 15 known Huret discrepancies and no gains or changed mappings before and after.

Early GS source follow-up, 2026-11-23: searched for explicitly GS-coded 1994–97 maker material and bike fitments. The earliest dated maker source located remains the October 1996 SI-52D0C English instruction above; the 1994 and 1996 catalogue entries are unsuffixed. [VeloBase](https://velobase.com/Pages/ViewComponent.aspx?ID=D90CAC77-A204-4E0C-A7A2-1F00469AF462) claims a 1996 model-year start but supplies no independent dated source for that claim. This does not establish either a 1994 or 1996 production start. Live readback confirms 4533 remains 1994–1998 and neither 4533 nor 10876 has linked bike specs. Seek a new dated coded source or original fitment lead before repeating the hunt; no DB or generator changes were made.

The 1994 Cinelli Argento Vivo source gives only "Shimano STX S.E."; live
specs 20155/20157 point to standard 6369, and 20159/20160 to later MC32
rear/crank rows 4537/1843. The 1994 Shimano Special Edition group lists
Chromica MC30-CH/MC31-CH models, but the bike's individual codes and working
year are not directly printed; the available original photo does not resolve
the crank code. These links are suspect, not yet proven wrong at component
level. MC32's 1993 starts are held pending that fitment review. No link or
generator change was made. Other holds include 1994 non-A versus 1995
-A hubs, the SP-M560/SP-M650 catalogue label discrepancy, and CS-HG70-C
gearing/configuration conflicts. P.21's SP-M560 label contrasts with p.18's
SP-M650 and the matching 325g/360mm specification; p.38's CS-HG70-C ab
11–19T differs from the 11–21T previously recorded on 7755 and the 1995 tables. The 1993 review subsequently recorded both dated configurations on 7755; the 1995 internal gearing conflict remains unresolved.

## Shimano "'95 Bicycle System Components — Dealer Sales & Support Manual", English (GB) — printed 07.1994 (disraeligears.co.uk, 88 images)

- `data_source` 174, label "Shimano Bicycle System Components 1995,
  European edition (printed 07.1994)". Source:
  https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_95.html
  (images `00_main_images/..._page_NN_main_image.jpg`, page N = printed page
  N, bound to shimano95.pdf; the full-page JPGs are 1560x2209 and the spec
  tables are readable at that size, not at the PDF render size). 1995 model
  year. Rear cover "Jul. 1994 Shimano Inc., 0794 Printed in Belgium XBC
  IZM", Shimano Europe B.V. (Nunspeet). Pages: 6-21 MTB (XTR, Deore XT,
  Deore LX, STX-RC, specs p16-17, feature charts p18-21), 22-37 STX,
  Alivio, Acera-X, Altus C90, MJ / MJ-II (specs p32-33, 37), 38-39
  700CX / 400CX, 40-41 Nexus (Inter-7, Inter-M, Revo-Shifter), 42-57 road
  (Dura-Ace, 600 Ultegra, 105SC, RX100, RSX, Exage 300EX; specs p52-55),
  58-73 technology pages (IG, Top-Swing, Dual SIS and Dual Control charts
  p66-67, BB chart p71, headset specs p73), 74-76 small parts and tools,
  77-85 SPD shoes, 86-87 Test Point, 88 rear cover.
- New for 1995: IG drivetrain (CS-IG60 / IG50, CN-IG50 / IG30, SGX-II
  chainrings) on STX-RC / STX / Alivio; Top-Swing front derailleur
  (Alivio, Acera-X, MJ-II, RSX); XTR M910 generation (RD / ST / FH / HB /
  SP-M910, FC-M900-II); Deore LX M565 / M566 (8-speed) and PD-M535; STX
  MC32 / MC33; Altus C90 (CT90, Pre-Select, Easy-Set BB-CT90); Acera-X
  M290; MJ-II (MJ05 / MJ06); RSX (A410 / A413) and Exage 300EX; Nexus
  Inter-7 hub with Inter-M roller brake and Revo-Shifter; SP-7410 seat post.
- year_to -> 1995 on 53 rows (ids 411, 442, 960, 961, 6145, 7670, 6146,
  6133, 6368, 3960, 3972, 3971, 3977, 4532, 3951, 7675, 7666, 7758, 7752,
  2471, 7756, 7759, 7753, 7760, 7754, 7707, 7706, 142, 7719, 7737, 1203,
  7668, 7671, 7762, 7755, 7567, 1195, 7574, 7568, 7566, 7665, 7614, 3527,
  7761, 3572, 7573, 7569, 3088, 5892, 7669, 4537, 1843, 2532): road brakes,
  levers and 8-speed shifters, SPD pedals, 700CX / 400CX, the UN / LP / CS
  bottom brackets, HG cassettes and chains, 105SC / 300EX hubs, CS-M900-8
  and SP-M900 (still listed in the XTR spec table next to the M910 parts),
  RD-A550 (RD-A550-GS), FD-A553, STX RD-MC32 and FC-MC32.
- Date fixes (all had velobase 1990-1990): 6364 ST-M738 -> 1994-1995
  (rewritten description); 2535 FD-MC33 -> 1995 (STX-RC is 1995, STX itself
  1994); 4531 RD-A410 GS retitled "RD-A410-SS / RD-A410-GS, RSX" (old title
  "Shimano RD-A410 GS, RSX", search_text rewritten) -> 1995; 6366 ST-A410
  and 1006 BR-A410 -> 1995 (RSX is "brand new" for 1995; SL-A410 was already
  1995-1998).
- 96 rows added, source_ref 174, group_id from the existing groups where
  they exist (XTR M900 60, Deore XT M737 136, Deore LX M560 Series 135, STX
  189, STX RC 190, Alivio 277, ALTUS 183, RSX 143, Exage 300EX 185, RX100
  57, Nexus 312) and three new groups Acera-X, MJ, MJ-II. year_from 1994
  only where the '94 entry above names the code (XT RD / FC / HB-M737,
  HP-M741 / M742, LX M563 / M564 ST, FC, HB, FH, HP, FC-MJ10 / MJ12, RX100
  FD / FC / FH / SL-A551); everything else 1995. Spec-table names that
  differ from the photo captions: FC-M900-ti (photo FC-M900-II), HG-M738-A
  (a typo for HB-M738-A), HB-J05 (photo HB-MJ05), FC-AS53 / RD-AS53 do not
  exist (misreads of FC-A550-T / RD-A550-GS).
- Not added (no DB category): SO-M900 / SO-M730 skewers, DF-M730 chain
  deflector, SM-SP17 cable guides, SM-ST10 / SM-CS50 / SM-ST74, tools,
  cable and casing, brake shoes, footwear and SPD cleats.
- At the original pass, descriptions of the 53 year-extended rows were not
  enriched from the 1995 spec tables; the later full-range follow-up below
  supersedes that historical status. 7558 SP-7410
  1986-1986 (source 73) is NOT a duplicate (the 1986 semi-oval B-type
  SP-7410, distinct from the 1994 SP-7410, id 5895) and was left; FD-M566
  (2494, no dates) and FD-M738 (2504, 1996-1998) not in this manual;
  RD-MC32 / FC-MC32 were 1993-1993 from velobase while the '95 manual says
  the STX group dates from 1994, so their year_from is doubtful; FH-M737 /
  FH-M737-A and HB-M737-A / HB-M738-A share a row or pair across ids;
  PD-M535 is "available early 1995" so its 1995 start is the catalogue
  year, not a firm date. Capped at 1994 (not in the '95 manual): RD / ST /
  FH / HB-M900, FC-M900 (1848), FH-M560 / HB-M560-F / FC-M560, FD-M563,
  ST-MC30 (6369), Altus C50 / Tourney / STX MC30 parts.

### 1995 specification follow-up — partial application, 2026-11-22

Live readback of the 53 historically year-extended IDs shows many have since
been enriched by later sources, so the historical list is not a current change
set. The original
[p.32](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_95_page_32_main_image.jpg)
gives FC-MC32 (1843) 42/32/22T, 94/58mm bolt circles and 170/175mm arms,
and RD-MC32 (4537) seven speeds, 11–32T sprockets, 20T maximum front
difference and 38T total capacity. Applied these two description enrichments
to 1843 and 4537 with source_ref 174; old descriptions were respectively
`Shimano FC-MC32, STX, Japan` and `Shimano RD-MC32, STX, Japan`. Readback
confirmed both descriptions, unchanged dates and provenance, lengths within
255 characters, and a repeat of the guarded updates affected zero rows. Their
1993 start dates remain unresolved and are not established by this table.
P.17 gives PD-M737 510g/pair, differing from the earlier 450g figure already
on 7666; the later full-range reconciliation below preserves both figures.
The source URLs for other pages follow the p.32 filename pattern.

### 1995 full-range reconciliation — applied and validated, 2026-11-22

The 1995 manual's MTB, junior, hybrid, Nexus, road and technical pages were
compared with current records. The earlier pass already covered most codes.
Applied 17 guarded description
updates (986, 1203, 2503, 2532, 3108, 4532, 5892, 5894, 6368, 7569, 7666,
7668, 7671, 7737, 7756, 7758, 7760), two end-year extensions (7751 and 7757,
1994 to 1995), and one conditional insert for FD-MJ12, component 12270,
source 174. All 19 updates and the insert affected one row each; readback
confirmed their values and no description exceeded 255 characters. An identical
rerun affected zero rows. Shimano duplicate report was unchanged; the new
FD-MJ12 and the two changed shifter codes appear in no live bike specifications.
The generator/live comparison was identical before and after, including its
15 pre-existing lost matches; no bike links changed. The quality report had
zero structural errors and the same review finding count before and after.

The missing FD-MJ12 is named in 1994 pp.36–37 and 1995 pp.36/66; 1994 p.37
specifies 14T capacity, 38T top ring, 28.0–28.6mm band, 68–73° chainstay,
painted steel band, chrome-plated steel cage and 155g. The 1995 chart pairs it
with FC-MJ12 38/28T. The two shifters are shown in 1995 pp.38 and 69; neither
has a linked bike spec or generator reference. P.39 misprints ST-C040 as
ST-M040. P.55 corrects 7671 cassette V/W ratios to 13–26 / 12–25T. The 1992
manual p.5 and 1995 p.17 both give CS-M900 Q with 16T, correcting the former
15T on 7668; the P weight is 332g in 1992 and 316g in 1995. The 1992 manual
p.6 and 1995 p.17 both give HP-M900 119g, correcting the former 170g attribution.
The 1991 p.55 and 1995 p.17 PD-M737 weights differ (450g and 510g per pair).

Manual conflicts: the 1994 p.39 FC-C700/C400 table gives
110/58mm bolt circles while 1995 p.39 prints 100/58mm; CS-HG70-C 11–21T on
1995 p.39 conflicts with the ac 11–28T table on p.17. The 1995 road table
prints BL/BR-1056 where photos and comparison pages use 1055; FH-HG50's 235g
appears to repeat the adjacent front hub figure. HB-6400 overlapping rows
still need identity review; the CS-HG70-8 versus CS-HG70-I configurations
were reviewed below. Do not infer unique identities from catalogue conflicts alone.

CS-HG70-8 / CS-HG70-I review, 2026-11-23: Keep 7762 and 9709 separate.
The 105SC CS-HG70-8 row 7762 lists road combinations including T 13–23T;
Shimano's [SI-R430A service instructions](https://www.sjscycles.com/Instructions/Shimano/Shimano_Speed_Cassette_Instructions.pdf)
identify the Deore LX CS-HG70-I as eight-speed with ah 11–28T and an 11–30T
combinations and CN-IG70, matching 9709. The distinct recorded configurations
support retaining both rows, without asserting that every similarly named
cassette is distinct or interchangeable. Live bike spec 1536 (Virata, 105 STI)
links to 7762: its uncoded 13–23T eight-speed value fits that row and the
generator's existing override; the exact cassette code is inferred. No bike
specs link to 9709. No database or generator changes were needed.

Follow-up evidence, 2026-11-23: Shimano's [FC-C400 exploded parts sheet EV-FC-C400-1317](https://si.shimano.com/de/pdfs/ev/FC-C400-1317/EV-FC-C400-1317.pdf) specifies 110 mm for the outer ring and 58 mm for the middle and inner rings. This is independent maker evidence for FC-C400; it does not establish the FC-C700 dimensions.

Live reconciliation, 2026-11-23: FC-C400 component 7759 already records
110 / 58 mm BCD, consistent with the parts sheet; no database change is
needed. A query found no bike specifications linked to 7759. The conflicting
1995 manual figure remains documented above.

FC-C700 reconciliation, 2026-11-23: Shimano's [EV-FC-C700-1316 parts sheet](https://si.shimano.com/de/pdfs/ev/FC-C700-1316/EV-FC-C700-1316.pdf) specifies 110 mm for the outer ring and 58 mm for the middle and inner rings. Live component 7753 already records 110 / 58 mm BCD, so no database change was needed. A query found no bike specifications linked to 7753.

HB-6400 overlap review, 2026-11-23: Live 3534 combines FH-6400-6/7 and
HB-6400-F (1988–1991); 9712 was a front hub dated 1996–1997 at that review. The 1993 manual subsequently confirmed 9712 by 1993, and its start was moved to 1993; both descriptions
give 100 mm spacing, a 108 mm axle and 212 g front weight. Shimano's
[1992 road table](https://vintagecannondale.com/cannondale/shimano/Shimano92.pdf)
still names HB-6400-F, while the 1996 catalogue records HB-6400 with the same
dimensions; these are evidence of overlap, not proof that every detail is
unchanged. A [parts list](https://manualzz.com/doc/53869848/shimano-hb-6400-hub-depan-exploded-view?lang=en)
names HB-6400-F and gives 100 mm spacing. Do not merge the front-only row into
the combined front/rear row on these similarities alone; compare period parts
keys or measured specimens and decide how to represent the shared front hub.
Seven hub specs link to 3534; none link to 9712. Spec 15055 is on the 1987
Peugeot Puymorens, whose catalogue lists Shimano 6400. Its year precedes
3534's 1988 start; the October 1987 Shimano brochure documents the 6400 range
for model year 1988. A separate [Peugeot archive](https://www.peugeotshow.com/1988fr.html)
places the W-suffixed Puymorens and the same associated range in its 1988
collection, whereas the loaded French catalogue was filed under 1987. This
raises a catalogue-wide edition dating question; it does not independently
prove the loaded scan's model year. Do not change 3534's dates on this flag
until the edition is established. No DB or generator changes were made.

## Shimano 1988 Bicycle System Components Dealer Catalog — January 1988 (Downloads/Shimano 88.pdf, 146 pages, scan, no text layer)

- Colophon "(c) Jan. 1988 by Shimano Industrial Co., Ltd." printed West
  Germany. Dealer parts catalogue, same format as the 1984 book: series
  spreads absent, straight into per-model exploded-parts pages grouped by
  category (Rear/Front Derailleurs, Shifting Levers, Front Chainwheels,
  Pedals, Hubs, Multiple Freewheels, Brake Arches, Brake Levers, Head
  Parts, Seat Pillars, Handle Stems, Seat Post QR, Chain Deflector,
  Chains, Tools, Fork Ends, Others). Entirely SIS-era (Dura-Ace 7400 down
  to Exage trail/Z, New Positron PPS, plus the older Positron SG-3C30
  three-speed hub); no overlap with the 1975/1982/1984 catalogues' part
  numbers.
- Years -> 1988 (15 rows, catalogue shows them still current): 4521
  RD-L532, 4519/4520 RD-L525 (both cage variants), 4529 RD-P500, 4523
  RD-M531 (year_to only, no year_from ever set), 2527 FD-M350/M351 (year_to
  only), 6128 SL-S434, 6127 SL-MS55 (year_to only), 3952 PD-T100, 3523
  FH-1050, 2218 MF-6208, 3520 CB-E110, 1004 BR-L490, 448 BL-AT50, 7154
  CN-UG20.
- Years corrected, year_from moved earlier (3 rows, DB claimed a start
  date *after* this catalogue's Jan 1988 date, which can't be right): 1809
  FC-MT60 (was 1989), 3971 PD-7401 (was 1990), 417 BL-6402 (was 1992).
- Added (51 rows, all 1988-1988, source_ref -> this catalogue's
  data_source row): RD-M450, RD-M350 (Rear Derailleurs); FD-A450, FD-M450
  (Front Derailleurs); SL-M450, SL-M350, SL-S441, SL-MS41, SL-MT36,
  SL-MT34, SL-AT50, SL-3S60, SL-P500 (Shifters); FC-M350 (Cranksets);
  BB-7600, BB-M450 (Bottom Brackets); PD-M731, PD-M450, PD-M350, PD-MX20,
  PD-E100 (Pedals); FH-MT60, HB-M450, FH-M450, SG-3C30, FH-7400-6/7,
  HB-7400-F (Hubs); MF-Z012, SS-7600 (Freewheels); BR-A450, BR-M450,
  BR-M451, BR-M350, BR-L570 (Brakes); BL-5002, BL-6400, BL-1052, BL-A450,
  BL-A453, BL-M450, BL-M350, BL-L330, BL-L331, BL-Z325, BL-Z326 (Brake
  Levers); HP-M730, HP-MT60, HP-M450, HP-M350 (Headsets); CN-7400, CN-6208
  (Chains).
- BR-L570 added rather than left unrepresented: DB had BR-L490 (49-type)
  but no 57-type companion, and the catalogue pairs them the same way
  BR-1050's 39-49/47-57 rows are already split in the DB, so the missing
  half was inserted rather than treated as covered by BR-L490 alone.
- FH-7400-6/7 / HB-7400-F deliberately NOT merged into the existing bare
  `Shimano FH-7400, Dura-Ace 7400 (freewheel)` row (id 3560, year 1980-1980,
  no weight/detail — the same "bogus single year, no real data" shape as
  the bare-brand exact-match bug fixed 2026-09-29 in the bike-spec
  generator). That row can't be confidently identified as the same
  sub-revision as this catalogue's plain FH-7400/HB-7400 (the DB's other
  7400-family rows are FH-7402/HB-7402 "Uniglide Only" 1985 and
  FH-7403/HB-7400 "Hyperglide Rear" 1991-96 — different numbers,
  different mechanism). Inserted fresh with the catalogue's own weights
  instead of guessing a merge; id 3560 left untouched.
- Not inserted (no component_category, out of scope): SQ-M730 (seat post
  quick-release) and DF-M730 (chain deflector) — accessory items with no
  matching category, same as fork ends/tools/cables/brazed-on parts.


## Shimano "Dura-Ace 10 Series Track Ensemble" launch sheet — late 1976 (labibleduvelocataloguesshimano.blogspot.com "SHIMANO 1976", 1 image)

- `data_source` 153. The blog's "SHIMANO 1976" page is not a catalogue but
  four loose images ("SHIMNANO GROUPSET 1-4", 640-1000px). Three are from
  catalogues already ingested: pp. 18 and 20 "Track Ensemble" (FC-7500 /
  SS-7500 / HB-7520 / HP-7500 / FE-SF10 / FE-UF10 and FC-7000 / HB-7020 /
  SS-7000 / CN-7000, the 1981-82 System Components numbering) and p. 39,
  the Dura-Ace 10 / Dura-Ace / EX / 600 EX / 600 / Deore range overview
  (1981 or 1982 catalogue). All 44 codes on those three were verified
  present with matching data; no source row created for them.
- The fourth image is a single undated Dura-Ace 10 track launch sheet:
  HA-310 hubs (240 / 303g, Duralumin Almite, BC33 x 24 sprocket, BC32 x
  24 left lock ring, 28/32/36h), GA-110 chainwheel with GB-100 BB set
  (75S Extra Super Duralumin, 10 x 3mm, 46-55T, 165 / 170, 167.5 by
  request), FA-210 sprocket (14 / 15 / 16T), QA-100 chain, plus the
  Dura-Ace 10 vs Dura-Ace weight chart (total 959g vs 1079g). Text:
  Nicholson "crowned World Professional Sprint Champion after
  successfully defending his 1975 title in Italy during September, 1976"
  and "Dura-Ace 10 Road System Components Ensemble will be introduced in
  1977" — so printed late 1976, before the Feb 1977 catalogue that had
  dated these rows.
- year_from 1977 -> 1976, source_ref -> 153, descriptions rewritten to
  carry the sheet's spec and the later drift: 7440 GA-110 / FC-7000
  (46-55T, 167.5 by request), 7449 QA-100 / CN-7000 (330g), 7452 HA-310 /
  HB-7020 (240 / 303g 1976-77, 225 / 295g from May 1978), 7461 FA-210 /
  SS-7000 (22-31g). GB-100 stays in 7144 "G-520 / GB-100".
- Also on the blog: the "SHIMANO 1975" page is pp. 15-32 of the Dec 1974
  "A Complete Line of Shimano" (data_source 24), verified 2026-10-10 as
  fully covered (55 codes, all weights and capacities matching); not
  loaded. Four titles there still break the `<code>, <name>` convention
  (2472 "Shimano 50, EC-100", 2454, 6123, 2709 lacking the comma) and ~20
  rows keep "1975 catalogue." / "Dec 1974 catalogue:" citations in their
  descriptions — optional cleanup, not done.
- Out of scope: FE-SF10 / FE-UF10 fork ends.

## Shimano "Bicycle Component" pocket catalogue — 1976 (cyclespeugeot.web.fc2.com "Part 13: Shimano in 1976", 8 scans, Japanese)

- Source: http://cyclespeugeot.web.fc2.com/reminiscence/shimano76.html. The
  page links `../catalog/shimano76/shimano76_01.jpg` to `_07.jpg`; `_08.jpg`
  also exists (the 600 Touring Ensemble) and `_09` redirects, so 8 scans,
  480px tall (244-732px wide), Japanese text, partly legible. Pocket-size
  folder: cover (John Nicholson, 1975 sprint world champion, on the Dura-
  Ace 10 / Dura-Ace / 600 emblems), racers page (Nicholson, Maertens, Stam,
  Shimano-Flandria and Shimano US teams), Dura-Ace 10 10mm-pitch track
  ensemble, Dura-Ace track ensemble (12.7mm), Dura-Ace road ensemble (3
  panels), 600 intro, 600 road ensemble, 600 touring ensemble.
- Undated; the page says 1976. Consistent with that: Nicholson's 1975
  title, Dura-Ace 10 (late 1976 launch sheet, data_source 153) with a road
  model "coming soon", and no Dura-Ace EX (1978).
- Codes read from the photo captions: Dura-Ace 10 GA-110 / GB-100, HA-310,
  FA-210, QA-100; track GA-100, HA-300, FA-200, UA-200, NC-100; road GA-200,
  GB-100, BA-100, MA-100, HA-100 / HA-200, FA-100 / FA-110, UA-100, EA-100,
  DB-100 Crane / DB-110 Crane GS, LA-100, LD-500; 600 DC-200 / DC-210,
  EC-200 / EC-210 ("Shimano 60" / "60 GS"), HB-100 / HB-200, GC-100 / GB-200,
  BB-300, BE-100 (cantilever), MB-200, LB-180.
- **Diff on 2026-10-17: every code already has a row, no years or
  descriptions changed, no rows added or deleted, no data_source created**
  (source_ref untouched everywhere). Matches: 7440, 7144, 7452, 7461, 7449,
  1812, 7124, 7125, 7126, 1816, 3556, 3554, 2219, 2220, 3098, 988 / 989, 433,
  2506, 4498, 4499, 6162 (L-284 / LA-100), 7113, 4462, 7110, 2474, 7112,
  3531, 3530, 1786, 7145, 964, 7127, 7130, 7114. Existing years (1973 or 1975
  to 1979 and later) already contain 1976; EC-200 / EC-210 end at 1976, still
  compatible. The weights that were legible agree (MB-200 160g pair, GA-110
  arm 239g). Existing descriptions come from the 1974 / 1977 / 1978
  catalogues and are fuller than this scan, so nothing was rewritten.
- The page claims the 600 series was introduced in this catalogue, but the
  600 codes (DC-200, EC-200, HB-100, GC-100, BB-300, MB-200) are already
  dated 1975 from the Dec 1974 catalogue (data_source 24), so 600 predates
  1976; years left as they are.
- Out of scope: fork ends NB-100 / NB-120 / NC-100 / ND-100 / NF-100, cable
  guides and stoppers KA-/KB-, outer band KA-210, pump holder KD-100, the
  600 road parts kit.
- Unresolved: exact codes on the 600 touring panel (DC-210 / EC-210, BE-100
  cantilever, NB-120 / NF-100 fork ends) were read at 480px and the 600 road
  caliper is read as BB-300 from the caption; spec text is not reliable.

## Shimano "'74 Catalogue" (SHIMANO CATALOGUE '74 商品カタログ), Japanese domestic edition — 1974 (cyclespeugeot.web.fc2.com "Part 80: Shimano in 1974", 12 scans), data_source id 178

- Source: http://cyclespeugeot.web.fc2.com/reminiscence/shimano74.html;

  8-11 brakes, pp. 12-13 freewheels, pp. 14-15 hubs, p. 16 spoke protectors,

  small spec print is not. Undated beyond "'74"; Eagle D-350 to D-380 are
  already the renumbered codes, so at least as late as the Jun 1974 export
  catalogue (data_source 65). A different printing from that English export
  edition, with a domestic numbering in places.

  D-600 / 601 / 610 / 611 (4437, 4438), Tourney D-700 / 701 / 710 / 711
  (7368, 7369), Eagle D-350 to D-380 (7418-7421), Lark D-210 / D-220 / D-280
  (7373, 7374, 4439), Titlist front E-304 (7376), Thunder Bird E-103 / E-104
  and GPO E-113 / E-114 (7377, 2461 already list those codes), levers
  L-223 / L-224 (7378), L-263 to L-266 (7381), L-273 to L-276 (7380), CL
  L-345 (7424), L-110 and L-120 (7393, 7389), brake levers M-410 / M-310 /
  M-110 / M-120 / M-210 / M-220 (7413, 7414, 7409, 7410), B-700 (7408), B-110
  / B-120 (7407), coaster C-100 (7406), hubs H-710 / H-810, H-720 / H-820,
  H-700 / H-800, H-300 / H-400 (7398-7400, 7402), T-200 (same 1100g, 28 / 36H
  spec as 7403 T100).
- year_to 1973 -> 1974, source_ref 60 -> 178: 7383 L231-L238 Short Lever
  (only L-233 / L-234 down tube and L-235 / L-236 stem listed) and 7379
  L241-L248 Long (only L-243 / L-244 / L-245 / L-246, the "New Long Lever").
  Both descriptions note which versions were still listed.
- 7408 B700 disc brake: description only, adds rear B-700, front B-701 and
  disc hub H-190 (source_ref -> 178).
- Inserted (1974-1974, UUID source_id, group_id NULL): 8766 L-293 / L-294 /
  L-296 Unishift (one-way clutch; later LB-200 code). This catalogue-only
  row 8766 and LB-200 row 7119 were consolidated into specimen 6129 in the
  approved 2026-11-17 review below. Other insertions:
  8767 L-545 / L-546 New Long Stem (long blades, 7/8in; L-535 / L-536 short
  versions are already in 7385), 8768 D-290 Lark Mini (mini-cycle, about
  190g), 8769 H-900 Combi 4-speed / Unit hub, 8770 D-702 mini-cycle disc
  brake.
- Not inserted (decided 2026-10-17): GT-500 L-431, GT-7 L-441, GT-5 L-451
  and GT-5 console (3S) L-481 (unclear against the older L411 / L422 / L461 /
  L472 consoles), EM lever L-169 / L-179, trigger L-152 (predecessor of
  LD-200), Skylark D-100 / D-110 (probably the D160 Sky Lark, 7375, under
  different codes), Tourney B-231 auto-adjust (likely BB-210, 1013, whose
  "new" is 1975) and the C-220 / C-230 / C-240 / C-250 / C-260 side-pull
  codes (against export B-220 / B-230, 7411), freewheels F-716 to F-724
  (against export F916-F924, 7396) and the F-102 to F-113 / F-204 / F-205 /
  F-235 multis (against F200-F512, 7397): the domestic numbering differs
  from the export editions and the mapping is a guess.
- Out of scope: spoke protectors P-100 / P-210 / P-310 / P-311, tools X-101
  / X-102 / X-203 / X-301 / X-302 / X-401, the Dura-Ace photo spread.
- Unresolved: whether the domestic D-100 / D-110 Skylark, B-231 and GT
  console codes are separate parts or renumbers.

## Shimano "105 Technical Information" sheets (1983), plus New 600 EX dealer catalogue pages — velo-pages.com via Wayback, data_source id 183

- Source: velo-pages.com Gallery2 items 7179-7234 (the TODO's "Shimano 105
  technical information (1983), 14 images"), read through the Wayback
  Machine. Query tip: the CDX index canonicalises URLs and sorts the query
  parameters, so search `velo-pages.com/main.php?g2_itemId=<prefix>` with
  `matchType=prefix`, not `g2_view=core.DownloadItem&g2_itemId=...` (that
  form silently returns 0 rows even for archived items). Each page is three
  ids (original, thumbnail, resize); image bytes come from
  `web.archive.org/web/<timestamp>id_/<original url>`.
- The range holds **two documents**. 105 Technical Information (5 pages
  archived at usable size, the rest only as thumbnails): 7179 (1275px) and
  its 495px copy 7181 = Derailleur Set FD-A105 / RD-A105 / SL-A105 with the
  front derailleur parts list (items 1-7, interchangeability with 600EX,
  Deore-XT, Deore, Altus); 7185 specs and front derailleur assembly; 7191
  rear derailleur and chain assembly; 7197 front chainwheel FC-S105 /
  FC-S125 parts; 7200 FC specs, BB and crank assembly, tools TL-FC20 /
  TL-FC30. From 7210 (1264px cover, "ENGLISH (U) Dealer Catalog") the New
  Shimano 600 EX dealer catalogue with Service Plus parts lists (7216 intro,
  7219 p. 2, 7222 RD-6207 / RD-6207GS, 7225 RD-6207 exploded view, 7228
  FD-6207, 7231 FD-6207 exploded view, parts-list code 8310-US94A).
- Dating: 1983; the 600 EX pages carry print code 8310 (Oct 1983) and the
  105 Golden Arrow is the 1984 range, the same material as the New 600 EX
  brochure (data_source 71).
- Diff on 2026-10-17: the DB already matched almost everything, because the
  brochure pass (71) had rewritten these rows. Matched, no change: FD-A105
  2466 (14T, 115g), SL-A105 6132 (63g), BB-3L11 / BB-3P11 123 (116 / 119mm,
  70mm shell 119 / 122mm), RD-6207 / GS 4470 / 4469 (195g / 217g), FD-6207
  2481 (18T, 110g, 28 / 28.6mm clamps). The 600 EX dealer pages repeat the
  brochure and add only spare parts lists. No new rows, no year changes.
- Updated (descriptions only, source_ref -> 183): 4452 RD-A105 (brochure
  233g against the technical sheet's 8.7oz / 247g, both named; capacity 13T
  front difference, 28T rear, 28T total; Hexagon-Release), 2466 FD-A105

- Not reachable: the other pages of the 105 technical information (lever

- Does not settle the Shimano gaps in the TODO: nothing here names BB-UN72,

## Shimano "'96 Bicycle System Components — Dealer Sales & Support Manual", English (E) — printed 07.1995 (disraeligears.co.uk, 92 images)

- `data_source` 277, label "Shimano Bicycle System Components 1996, English (E)
  dealer sales and support manual (printed 07.1995)". Source:
  https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_96.html
  (images `00_main_images/..._-_96_page_NN_main_image.jpg`, image N = printed
  page N; the 1560x2209 JPGs are legible read whole, no cropping needed).
  Rear cover "(c) Jul. 1995 Shimano Inc., 0795 Printed in Belgium XBC IZM",
  Shimano Europe. 1996 model year. Pages: 6-11 functions for 1996, 12-27 XTR /
  Deore XT / Deore LX / STX-RC (specs 22-23, feature charts 24-27), 28-39 STX,
  Alivio, Acera-X, Altus, MJ-II, Tourney 30 / 22 / 15 (specs 36-39), 40-45
  Nexus (specs 45), 46-61 road (Dura-Ace 7400, 600 Ultegra 6400, 105SC, RX100,
  RSX, 300EX; specs 56-59, charts 60-61), 62-73 function pages (BB chart 73),
  74-79 cables, pads, tools, 80-89 SPD shoes and pedals.
- Duplicate / partial copy, checked 2026-10-26 and not loaded: bikalog.org
  https://bikalog.org/api/view/Shimano/1996/shimano96.pdf is 9 group-photo
  pages (printed 27-35) of the US edition of this manual (same photos,
  "aluminum" spelling, different pagination), no specs or imprint.
- **New for 1996 (stated)**: XTR "completely re-designed" (M950, differential
  plate FD, V-brake); V-brake on XTR and XT; Rapidfire SL (XTR, XT, LX,
  STX-RC, STX); EZ-Fire (Acera-X, Altus); Top-Swing on all MTB groups; Silent
  Clutch FH-R050 (STX-RC, STX, Alivio); Easy-Set cantilevers; 7.1mm IG chains
  CN-IG90 / 70 / 51 / 31; SP-40 cable. vs '95: XTR M910 RD / FH / HB gone
  (ST-M910, CS-M900-I, SP-M900, HP-M900 still listed); Altus C90 RD / FD / FC /
  BB -> CT91; Acera-X M290 RD / FD / FC -> M291; ST-MC30 -> MC34; Nexus SB-7R40
  -> SB-7S40. Road range unchanged (Dura-Ace still 8-speed 7400).
- **year_from 1997 -> 1996 (58 rows, all inserted 1997-1997 by the '97 pass)**:
  119, 2494, 2536, 9602, 9603, 9605, 9606, 9607, 9609-9617, 9619-9621, 9632-
  9635, 9638, 9639, 9641, 9642, 9645-9653, 9655, 9656, 9658, 9660, 9662, 9663,

### 1996 catalogue triage — 2026-11-23

- [Page 15](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_96_page_15_main_image.jpg) specifies CS-M950 P-group titanium sprockets 24/28/32T, but ak-group titanium sprockets 26/30T. At triage, component 9615 described the three largest as titanium without distinguishing the groups; the full-range reconciliation below corrected it.
- Pages 15 and 25 identify ST-M950 and ST-M739 combined levers, not the similarly numbered SL-M950 and SL-M739 shift-only records (9608 and 9618); the checked pages do not establish earlier dates for those records.
- This was a sample review; no database changes were made. The remaining scope of the earlier pass is listed above.

### 1996 full-range reconciliation — applied and validated 2026-11-23

- Reviewed the component charts and associated group pages 12–45 and 46–61,
  bottom bracket chart p73 and pedals p88 against current Shimano records. Shoes,
  cleats, tools, cables, binders and other small accessories have no applicable
  component category. The corresponding catalogue page images and tables are
  identified in the earlier 1996 entry above.
- Applied 71 updates and inserted 1996 IG crank variants FC-M737-IG 12340 and
  FC-M563-IG 12341, source 277. The maker distinguishes these IG-compatible
  chainwheels from existing HG-compatible FC-M737 8669 and FC-M563-S/B 8677;
  retain the pairs separately. Date changes moved ST-1055-T 10887 and round
  FC-A300 10859 from 1998 to 1996; no later start changes or deletes.
- Examples: CS-M950 9615 distinguishes P and ak titanium sprockets; FD-M950
  9612, RD-MC36 9634, RD-MC34 9648 and RD-M291 9651 preserve differing printed
  1996 and 1997 specifications rather than conflating them. Those 1997 maker
  figures are from the separately registered 1997 manual, source 275. FC-CT91
  9660 preserves its 1996/1997 chainring conflict. SP-7400-A 5893 records the
  1996 SP-7400-B semi-oval name; the older semi-oval SP-7410 7558 identity
  remains unresolved.
- The 1996 p59 and p73 bottom-bracket charts contradict each other on the
  RSX FC-A410/A413 chainlines and the A413 LP26/LP20 pairing; p59 agrees with
  the 1997 p63 chart. RX100 FC-A550-T 1996 p59 gives 47.5mm chainline, p73
  gives 45mm under the incorrect FC-A553 code, and 1997 p63 gives 45mm.
  Do not silently overwrite 9602/9603 on these discrepancies. The M565-E,
  MC33-E and M900-C brake suffix crosswalks, the SP-M950/SP-M900 inconsistency,
  SL-A400 duplicate candidate, and RD-MC14 photo/chart conflict remain open
  identity questions, not automatic new rows or merges.
- The 1996 p86 and p88 conflict over whether PD-M323 cleats are supplied.
  Printed shoe and cleat ranges were examined for scope; no footwear category
  exists.
- Validation: all 71 updates matched their intended values and source on
  readback; the two new rows have the correct category, group and source.
  A repeat of the guarded SQL affected zero rows. The Shimano exact and near
  duplicate reports found no new crank pair; the IG/HG pairs were reviewed
  separately as above. Exact model-code searches found no corresponding loaded
  bike specs. Generator/live comparison covered 118 catalogues and 24,829
  specs: no gained or changed mappings and 15 unrelated Huret Rival differences;
  no bike links were changed. The whole-database quality report found zero
  integrity errors. Historical values for the updated rows follow below.

## Shimano "'97 Bicycle System Components — Dealer Sales & Support Manual", English (U) — printed 09.1996 (disraeligears.co.uk, 94 images)

- `data_source` 275, label "Shimano Bicycle System Components 1997, English (U)
  dealer sales and support manual (printed 09.1996)". Source:
  https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_97.html
  (images page_001..094, image N = printed page N). Contents: MTB groups 20-47
  (spec tables 30-35, 38-47), road groups 48-66 (Dura-Ace 50-53 and 60-65, Track 66),
  Separate Components 67, Nexus 68-76, SPD 77-85, replacement parts and tools
  86-89. 1997 model year; first catalogue in the log after the '95 manual
  (`data_source` 174). Spec tables read with the images at full size; the
  shifter / brake / hub weights below are as printed.
- **New for 1997**: Dura-Ace 7700 (9-speed, hollow FC-7700, BB-7700 pipe billet
  spindle, ST-7700, CN-7700, 9-speed CS-7700 / FH-7700 / RD-7700); XTR M950,
  Deore XT M739 and Deore LX M569 (V-brake, separate Rapidfire SL, 4-arm
  cranks, aluminium-carrier cassettes); FH-R080 / FH-R050 roller clutch
  freehubs; all-new Alivio MC14 / MC16; STX-RC 8-speed (SL-MC38, CS-HG60-8-I,
  FH-MC38); 7-speed Tourney 22 / 30 and Tourney 05; Nexus Inter-S shifters and
  Inter-M roller brakes. 600 Ultegra (6400), 105-SC, RX-100, RSX and 300EX are
  carried over.
  **Corrected by the '96 manual (`data_source` 277, entry above)**: XTR M950,
  Deore XT M739 (V-brake), Alivio MC14, STX-RC MC36, STX MC34, Tourney 30 / 22,
  Nexus Inter-S ST-7S20 / 4S20, Inter-M brakes / hubs and SG-7C21 / 4R31 / 4C30
  are all in the 1996 manual, so 58 of this pass's 1997-1997 rows now start in
  1996. Still genuinely 1997: Dura-Ace 7700, Deore LX M569, Alivio MC16,
  STX-RC MC38, Tourney 05, FH-R080 (absent from the '96 manual).
- **Updated, year_to -> 1997 only (111 rows, each guarded on its old year_to)**:

  92, FD-CT92-E, SB-CT91 / 95, BR-CT91, FC-CT91 / 92, FH / HB-RM40; MJ-II
  FD-MJ07-E; Tourney RD-TY30 / TY22, RD-TY05, FD-TY32 / TY22, FD-TY05, SL-TY37 /

- **Printed inconsistencies** (descriptions follow the spec table): p62 pairs

  exists in the manual); BB-UN52 weights 427g / 415g equal the FH-1056 /

  (spec); RD-MC14 / MC16 capacity "37T" and RD-MC34 "29T" are as printed.
- **Not in this manual**: BB-UN73 (121) and BB-LP27 (120) appear nowhere (only
  BB-LP26), so both stay as they were; the BB-UN71 / UN51 and BB-CS21 / CS11
  rows were not extended. The later review below found BB-CS11 in a p46 photo

- **Left**: SQ-M900 / SQ-M730 seat binders, DF-M730, SM-SP18, cables, tools,

  (the 1997 HB-6400 front hub is a later part on the same code).
- **Page check, 2026-10-26** (two fresh readers re-compared every MTB, low-end
  and Nexus description with the page images; 17 rows corrected, all
  `source_ref` 275): FD-M566 (2494) is not top-swing and has no printed
  weight; ST-M567 (6361) and ST-MC36 are Rapidfire SL, not Plus; BL-M950 and
  BL-M600 lose the 159g (printed for BL-M739 only); RD-MC36 / RD-MC38 (4538)
  pulleys are sintered steel bushings on the p30 table while the p29 text says
  chromized (page contradicts itself); PD-M747 (3969) lost "lightweight
  compact"; BR-M950 "F and R" dropped; BB-UN52 gained the MM110 (110.5mm)
  version; BB-LP26 spindle is 110.5mm; BR-IM31 also comes without a cooling
  disc; RD-TY30 has one 34T / 20T figure and the GS / SS split belongs to
  RD-TY22; RD-MC14 / MC16 Teflon is on the pins; FD-MJ07-E "Dual SIS" removed.
  CS-M738-I is CS-M737-I in the p32-33 tables. Confirmed: CN-IG90 / IG70 /
  IG51 / IG31 are 7.1mm (p33), and the Nexus p76 weights sit under the right
  columns.

### 1997 manual remaining-range triage — 2026-11-23, historical

- The original pass was substantial, and the 1996 full-range follow-up above
  enriched many shared rows. A sample review still finds useful 1997-only
  detail: [p60](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_97_page_60_main_image.jpg)
  specifies SL-7700 as a braze-on, polished aluminium, 9-speed down-tube
  shifter using SIS-SP40 casing; at triage component 6167 only said "9-speed down
  tube shift lever". The same page gives FD-7700-B/F 2514 replaceable cage,
  61–66° chainstay angle and 28.0–28.6mm band size, absent at triage. Both
  descriptions were updated in the full-range follow-up below.
- [p32](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_97_page_32_main_image.jpg)
  identifies FC-M737-I and FC-M563-I as IG-compatible, with gearing and
  weights matching 1996 FC-M737-IG 12340 and FC-M563-IG 12341 respectively.
  The full-range follow-up below establishes the suffix crosswalk and extends
  both rows to 1997; similar figures alone were not the basis for the change.
- [p66](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_97_page_66_main_image.jpg)
  pairs FC-7600 with BB-7610 in 1997; at triage 1823 named only BB-7600.
  The full-range follow-up preserves both pairings by edition. This triage was
  a sample review and made no database changes.

### 1997 full-range follow-up — applied and validated 2026-11-23

- Compared the maker's 1997 MTB, road, track, Tourney, Nexus, tandem and pedal
  specifications against live rows after the 1996 reconciliation. MTB pages
  20–35, lower groups 36–47, road/track 48–66, separate components 67, Nexus
  68–76 and SPD pedals 84 were reviewed. Shoe/cleat pages 77–83/85 and cables,
  tools, binders and small accessories are outside component categories.
- Applied **41 guarded updates, no inserts or deletes**, using source 275.
  Original values follow below for a deliberate restoration. All descriptions
  fit their fields. No title, category, group or search-text changes were made.
- Nine date changes: 10867 FD-M567-E and 10868 FD-M739-E start in
  1997 rather than 1998; 7673 SP-M650, 9707 BL-TY21, 9708 FC-TS21,
  9711 BL-IM40/IM30, 12339 SL-TY30-L and IG cranks 12340/12341 extend to
  1997. The 1997 p32/33 tables explicitly cross-reference FC-M737-I and
  FC-M563-I to the -IG designations, so the 1996 IG rows do not need duplicate
  aliases. SL-TY30-L's 1994 friction description and 1997 SIS-pair statement
  are retained as source-specific claims, not asserted to be identical designs.
- The 1997 p82 says SM-SH51 cleats are included with PD-M323, while p84 says
  cleats are not included. Do not resolve the contradictory package contents
  from this manual alone. Separate-component p67 models already have matching
  rows and no additional change is proposed from the reviewed specifications.
- 9600 CN-7700 incorrectly said the narrow chain had the same overall width as
  an 8-speed chain; p52 attributes that statement to the cassette cluster.
  9615 CS-M950: 1996 p15 explicitly gives two titanium sprockets in the ak
  group; 1997 p23 gives a general three-largest statement alongside both groups.
  The applied description attributes both without claiming which printing is correct.
  The earlier 1996 full-range text should be read with this 1997 conflict.
- Other held questions: p46 photo labels BB-CS11 while p47 specifies BB-CS12;
  8704 CS-IG50 group codes differ within the 1997 tables; 8697 HP-MC31 thread
  differs from the earlier catalogue; RD-MJ05-GS and FD-CT90-E specifications
  differ between printings. The applied descriptions identify those conflicts
  rather than silently selecting one. Crosswalks for BR-M900-C, BR-M565-E and
  BR-MC33-E remain unresolved. The earlier 1997 statement that BB-CS11 is
  absent applies to the specifications table, not the p46 photo caption.
- Validation: all 41 rows matched intended and preserved fields on readback;
  the identical SQL rerun affected zero rows. The Shimano near-duplicate report
  had zero candidate pairs both before and after. Generator/live comparison
  covered 118 loaded catalogues and 24,829 specs before and after, with zero
  gained/changed mappings and the same 15 unrelated Huret Rival losses. No
  affected date-change row or exact model-code text had a loaded bike link in
  the scoped query, and no bike links were changed. The whole-database quality
  report returned zero integrity errors. Task scratch files were removed after
  readback and idempotency validation.

#### Previous values for the 1997 full-range updates

- 1209 (1996–2004, source 275): 9-speed HG cassette, titanium on the largest 2-3 sprockets, aluminium alloy sprocket carrier, champagne gold and titanium silver, 159.5g (12-23T); 187g measured (12-27T)
- 1823 (1986–1997, source 173): Track crank, high-tech grey anodized ring, Ni-Cr-Mo axle, 1/8 or 3/32in, 165-175mm, 605g (48T, 170mm); BB-7600. Rings: 46-52T (42-45/53-54T option) in 1986; 44-55T in 1994.
- 2514 (1997–2000, source 275): Dual SIS front derailleur for SG-X chainrings, nickel plated aluminium chain cage with widened mounting points, 15T capacity, band (-B) or braze-on (-F), 72.5g (-F)
- 3535 (1992–1997, source 83): Shimano FH-6402-HG, 600 Ultegra (8-speed HG), Japan. 8-speed HG freehub, new for 1992, lip seal, Uni-Balance, 130mm, axle 141mm, 28-36h, buff anodized, 426g with QR (Aug 1991).
- 3566 (1997–2000, source 275): 9 and 8-speed freehub, 130mm over locknut, 141mm axle, titanium freewheel body, 375g; HB-7700 front hub 100mm over locknut, 108mm axle, 177.5g. Labyrinth and contact seals, stainless steel balls, 36/32/28/24H (aero type 32/28/24/18/16/12H)
- 3972 (1993–1999, source 366): Shimano PD-7410, Dura-Ace (SPD road), Japan. SPD competition road pedal, new for 1993, 36 deg clearance, cartridge axle, cleat retention indicator and adjuster, alloy, Cr-Mo axle, 313g pair (Jul 1992). 315g measured.
- 3977 (1990–1997, source 275): Shimano PD-A550, RX100, Japan. Road pedal, 34 deg clearance, cartridge axle, aero, sealed, barrel-finish alloy body, resin clip, 400g pair with L clip; also on Exage 500EX (407g) (1990 manual). 366g measured.
- 4486 (1995–1998, source 277): 8-speed rear derailleur, Advanced Light Action, Teflon-coated pivot pin bushings, 32T max, 11T min, 20T front difference, 38T capacity, electrostatic painted aluminium, chrome plated steel pulley cage, 285g. 293g measured
- 4506 (1997–2001, source 275): 9 or 8-speed SIS rear derailleur, 11T sealed bearing pulleys in Zytel, extension link spring and Teflon-coated pivots, 26T max, 11T min, 26T capacity, 192.5g; 8-speed lever use by changing the cable mount position
- 5895 (1994–2000, source 277): Single-butted thin-wall aluminium seat post, round, 26.8 / 27.0 / 27.2mm, 270mm long, 195g; 40g lighter and 50mm longer than the SP-7400
- 6167 (1997–2003, source 275): 9-speed down tube shift lever
- 6168 (1996–1999, source 353): 9-speed bar-end shift lever, 178g
- 6358 (1992–1997, source 83): Shimano ST-6400, 600 Ultegra (Dual Control), Japan. STI road brake / shift lever, new for 1992, 8-speed SIS, Super SLR, Light Action, sealed, used with BR-6403, 23.8-24.2mm, 542g (Aug 1991). 523g measured.
- 7673 (1992–1996, source 277): Cr-Mo seatpost, pearl bright finish, 26.4/26.6/26.8/27.0/27.2mm; 280/330/360mm, 360mm only in 27.2mm; 325g (360mm). New for 1992 and listed with Deore LX in 1996.
- 8682 (1995–1999, source 366): SPD pedal with both pedal surfaces, black anodized aluminum body, chrome plated steel plate, BC 9/16" x 20TPI, 410g pair
- 8697 (1994–1999, source 173): Cartridge headsets, 37.6mm stack. HP-MC30 BC 1" x 24TPI, 170g; HP-MC31 BC 1-1/8" x 26TPI, 190g
- 8704 (1995–1999, source 277): 7-speed IG cassette, ag 11-13-15-18-21-24-28T, silver. 1997 lists aj 11-28T and am 11-30T. For use with the IG system only.
- 8716 (1995–1997, source 275): Easy-Set Top-Swing front derailleur mounted on the bottom bracket, 14T top-low capacity, 8T top-middle, 38T top gear, 28.0-28.6 / 31.8mm clamp, Dual SIS
- 8734 (1995–1997, source 277): 6-speed SIS rear derailleur, Advanced Light Action, 11T min. GS 24T max, 20T max front difference, 34T capacity, dropout hanger; SS 28T max, 13T difference, 28T capacity.
- 9599 (1997–1999, source 353): 9-speed Dual Control Lever, double front. Precision main lever bearing, anatomical bracket, levers closer to the bar with a longer release lever, in-line cylindrical Optical Gear Display, engineering plastics in the bracket (100g pair saving), 380g (pair)
- 9600 (1997–1999, source 353): 9-speed narrow HG chain, outer plate width reduced from 7.30mm to 6.60mm (external width 0.7mm less) with tighter tolerances, same overall width as an 8-speed chain, 304g (116 links)
- 9608 (1997–1997, source 275): Rapidfire SL shift lever set without brake lever, 8-speed, triple front, no Optical Gear Display
- 9610 (1996–1998, source 365): V-brake, Parallel-Push linkage, extra long cartridge pads (M-70R2) with optional pads for ceramic rims, low-flex design, ball bearing and DU bushing pivots, mounts to standard bosses, 375g; lighter than the original XTR cantilever
- 9615 (1996–1998, source 277): 8-speed HG cassette, forged aluminium carrier, champagne gold and titanium silver. P 12-14-16-18-21-24-28-32T (232g), titanium 24/28/32T; ak 11-13-15-17-20-23-26-30T, titanium 26/30T.
- 9618 (1997–1998, source 365): Rapidfire SL shift lever set without brake lever, 8-speed, triple front, no Optical Gear Display
- 9621 (1996–1998, source 365): 8-speed rear derailleur, cold-forged aluminium, four Teflon-coated link bushings, self-centering sealed ceramic upper pulley. RD-M739-GS 32T max, 11T min, 22T front difference, 33T capacity; -SGS 20T difference, 39T capacity, 250g actual.

- 9642 (1996–1998, source 365): Top-swing front derailleur, 18T capacity, 8T top-middle, 42T top gear, clamp 28.0-28.6 / 31.8 / 34.9mm, chainstay angle 63-66 / 66-69 degrees
- 9657 (1997–1997, source 275): Bottom-bracket mounted front derailleur, 18T capacity, 8T top-middle, 42T top gear
- 9661 (1997–1997, source 275): 7-speed freehub, 130/135mm over locknut, 36H; HB-RM40 front hub 100mm over locknut, 36H
- 9674 (1996–1999, source 366): 7-speed internally geared hub with pedal-activated coaster brake, ratios 0.632 to 1.545 (244%), 169.5mm axle, 127mm over locknut, 36H, 16-22T sprockets, 1780g
- 9676 (1996–1999, source 366): 4-speed internally geared hub with pedal-activated coaster brake, ratios 1.000 to 1.843 (184%), 165.6mm axle, 123.5mm over locknut, 36H, 1680g
- 9707 (1994–1996, source 173): M-System compatible brake lever, 22.2mm clamp; 2.5-finger in 1994, 3-finger in 1996. Also left-hand lever for Nexus SB-4S30 / ST-4S20.

- 9709 (1996–1998, source 365): 8-speed HG cassette for IG chain, ah group 11-12-14-16-18-21-24-28T, steel sprockets, silver, 326g; for FH-M565

- 10867 (1998–1998, source 365): E-type top-swing Advanced Light Action Dual SIS front derailleur, 20T capacity, 42T top ring.
- 10868 (1998–1998, source 365): E-type top-swing Advanced Light Action Dual SIS front derailleur, 22T capacity, 42/44T top ring.

## bikeforums.net "1997-98 Shimano catalog scans" thread 1270806 — checked 2026-11-02, nothing loaded

- https://www.bikeforums.net/classic-vintage/1270806-1997-98-shimano-catalog-scans.html
  (site returns 403 to automated fetches; the user saved the 14 gallery images
  to ~/Downloads/Shimano98/). Image 1 photographs the covers of both the '97
  and the '98 "Shimano Bicycle System Components, Dealer Sales & Support
  Manual", English (U). The other 13 are interior pages: XTR M950 photo, road
  spec tables 60-65, 600 Ultegra 54-55, 105-SC 56, Dura-Ace 50-51, Track 66.
- **These are '97 pages (`data_source` 275), not '98 ones.** The page numbers
  match the '97 manual, the 105-SC / 6400 / 8-speed range and "New Dura-Ace"
  text are the 1997 line-up, the printed errors the '97 entry records are all
  here (p62 FC-6410, BB-UN52 427g / 415g, p55 FD-6402, BR-1055 photo), and
  p64 is identical to disraeligears' '97 page 64. Nothing here dates anything
  to 1998, so no year_to was extended. The '98 manual's interior is still
  unseen; BB-UN73 / BB-LP27 (121 / 120) and the 1998 range remain open.

- Follow-up 2026-11-06: full 1998 and 1999 US consumer catalogues found and
  ingested as sources 365 / 366 below. These are separate from this gallery
  of 1997 dealer-manual pages. BB-LP27 is now attested; BB-UN73 remains absent.

## Shimano Bicycle Components 1998, US catalogue — source 365, loaded 2026-11-06

- Source: [Charlie Taylor archive](https://www.charlie-taylor.net/bikes/library/shimano/1998/00.htm).
  Full scans at `1998/large/98Shimano<page-pair>.jpg`, cover `00`, spreads
  `01-02` through `57-58`, final page `59`. Read all 31 images. Printed p59
  states copyright December 1997, publication 1297; catalogue/model year is
  1998. US edition (Shimano American Corporation / Canada warranty), country US.
- 47 new components, IDs 10848-10894, and 105 existing-row updates. New group
  327 DX (BMX) and 328 Nexave, both owned by Shimano. Added model-coded
  Nexave T400 parts, XTR M951 controls / Rapidfire Remote / reverse-action
  rear derailleurs and crank variants, later STX-RC controls and cranks,
  DX MX66 hubs and single sprocket, plus named mount and gearing variants.
- Bottom brackets: 120 BB-LP27 attested in Alivio and RSX tables, removing
  the inferred-date qualifier; kept 1998-1998 and 357g actual weight.
  Added 10848 BB-LP27-E as the separately listed E-type mounting version.
  XT still lists BB-UN72/-E; no BB-UN73 anywhere. Do not re-check this
  catalogue for UN73.
- Starts moved earlier: 10074 ST-6500, 10075 FH/HB-6500, 10076 CS-6500 and
  10077 PD-7700, all 1999 -> 1998. No valid earlier dates were moved later
  merely because the parts appear here. Year extensions apply to explicitly
  attested members of combined rows, not independently to every sibling code.
- Preserved existing combined families: FD-6503 -> 2538 FD-6500 family,
  FC-6503 -> 1847 FC-6500 family, HB-M738 -> 8670 HB-M737-A / HB-M738-A;
  updated the first two titles to include their triple codes, with search_text.
  CS-M900-I / CS-HG70-I map the existing 8-speed IG-compatible rows, not the
  similarly named road cassettes. Explicit mount/cage differences remain
  distinct. Seven-speed indexed SL-A400 is a new 300EX row, not the old

## BB-UN73 unsupported dating — evidence review 2026-11-15

- Before correction, row **121**, `Shimano BB-UN73`, had **1998-1998**, source_ref **1**,
  VeloBase GUID `746BA547-D186-4942-90B5-A285B69332A7`, and **zero bike links**.
  Its original description was `Shimano BB-UN73, Japan. Sealed cartridge bottom
  bracket, successor to BB-UN72 (1996-97); English 1.370 x 24 (velobase). Date
  inferred from the UN70 / UN71 / UN72 sequence; no catalogue seen.`
  Those dates came from our 2026-11-02 sequence inference, not manufacturer
  dating; the VeloBase record itself lists years N/A. They are not defensible
  as a supported production/availability range.
- **New introduction lead:** [Sheldon Brown / John Allen, Raleigh threading
  and interchangeability](https://www.sheldonbrown.com/raleigh26.html), section
  "Shimano UN72 With Phil Wood Rings", explicitly reports UN72 being replaced
  by UN73 for **model year 2003**, explaining the change from a removable
  right mounting ring to threads integral with the cartridge shell. This is
  a named technical author's report, not a recovered 2003 Shimano launch
  document. Keep that distinction when describing the start year.
- Re-downloaded, text-checked and visually read the official Shimano archive:
  - [2005 specifications, printed/PDF p.66](https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2005_SPECIFICATION.pdf#page=66),
    Bottom Bracket (ATB / NEXAVE), page dated **March 1, 2004**.
  - [2007 specifications, printed/PDF p.59](https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2007_SPECIFICATION.pdf#page=59),
    Bottom Bracket (ATB), page dated **February 20, 2006**.
  Both explicitly list **BB-UN73**, hollow non-splined spindle and BC1.37
  threads; the latter specifies steel spindle material. Both show 110 / 113mm
  spindles for 68 / 73mm shells and 122.5mm for a 68mm shell. These are
  positive manufacturer attestations for the **2005 / 2007 model-year** books;
  distinguish them from their earlier page revision dates and later PDF
  creation timestamps. The later listing disproves the recorded 1998-only
  range; it does not establish when production ended.
- **Applied 2026-11-15, user approved:** changed **1998-1998 -> 2003-2007** as an
  evidence-based model-year availability range, explicitly recording **2003
  as reported by Brown/Allen** and **2007 as the latest manufacturer catalogue
  attestation checked, not discontinuation**. Source_ref **1 -> 408**, new
  catalogue source `Shimano Specifications (2007 model year, revised 2006-02-20)`,
  citation `https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2007_SPECIFICATION.pdf#page=59`.
  The Brown/Allen introduction report remains separately cited above; it is
  not a fact established by the manufacturer's 2007 table.
- Replaced the sequence-derived description (including the unsupported
  "1996-97" chronology and "no catalogue seen" claim) with **231 characters**:
  `Sealed square-taper cartridge BB; hollow steel spindle, BC1.37 threads,
  68/73mm shells. 110/113mm axles for both shell widths; 122.5mm for 68mm.
  MY2003 introduction reported; 2007 is latest checked attestation, not
  discontinuation.` Title, component/GUID, brand/category/group and search_text
  are unchanged. No bike links or source CSVs were changed. Manufacturer
  confirmation of the introduction and a positive discontinuation statement
  remain open; do not turn these availability bounds into proven production dates.
- Validation: transactional guarded source INSERT plus component UPDATE each
  affected **one row**; second run affected **zero rows**. Readback confirmed
  source **408**, dates, description/length and zero bikes linked to 121.
  Compared all **20,600 live bike_spec/component links** unchanged. Re-generated
  **20,600 spec INSERT mappings from 93 local CSVs** in memory before/after:
  **zero mapping changes**, preserving existing `bike-update.sql`. No matcher
  or component identity changes. Restoration, only if deliberately justified:
  guard row 121 by its current 2003/2007 dates, new description and source 408,
  restore the original 1998/1998 description/source 1 above, and preserve its
  stable IDs. The bibliography source can remain registered for its evidence.
- **Worklist reconciliation 2026-11-17:** live readback confirms row 121
  still has 2003–2007 availability bounds, source 408, the qualified description
  above and no bike links. Database cleanup is complete; the remaining request
  is a source hunt for manufacturer-confirmed introduction/discontinuation
  evidence, now grouped with the other source hunts in TODO. No new source lead
  was identified in this reconciliation, so the recorded searches were not repeated.
- **Research scope / do not repeat:** the already-reviewed 1998/1999 consumer
  catalogues were not reopened. Searches covered c.2000-2003 manufacturer and
  service-document leads; no earlier dated Shimano UN73 listing recovered.
  Guessed official `2003_SPECIFICATION.pdf` / `2004_SPECIFICATION.pdf` paths
  were not retrievable (2004 returned 404 via curl). The Disraeli 2003 spare
  parts index is only six derailleur sheets, not a full bottom-bracket book.
  Search hits for **TL-UN73** refer to a tool and do not attest **BB-UN73**.
  Seller/weight-list entries labelled 2002 were not accepted as verified
  manufacturer launch evidence. A dated 2002/2003 Shimano catalogue/service
  page naming the actual BB is the useful next lead, not another model-number
  sequence inference. Official PDF downloads/renders were discarded after
  their page references and findings were recorded. Correction SQL, comparison
  script and baseline snapshots removed after readback and idempotency validation.


## BB-UN73 2003 manual acquisition lead — 2026-11-19, read-only

Followed a new archive-index lead for the remaining manufacturer dating task;
the previous 1998/1999 catalogue checks and rejected 2002 seller/weight-list
dates were not reopened. No component or source rows were changed.

- [VeloRoad thread 10321](https://veloroad.spb.ru/forum/viewtopic.php?f=14&t=10321), post by `andrey` dated 2007-01-23, lists BB-UN73 among the contents of a claimed 1999–2004 Shimano spare-parts collection. Its actual public archive link is `http://www.vast-arp.spb.ru/mozgj/ShimanoRoad.rar`, which returned HTTP 404. The model list is a holder/index claim, not a recovered maker page and not introduction evidence. No replacement archive was recovered.
- A more specific acquisition target is now verified from original document photographs: **Shimano 2003 Bicycle Components Trade Sales & Support Manual**, **English (U)**. [eBay item 326426841815](https://www.ebay.com/itm/326426841815), seller Hobby Time Seattle, exposes the [dated cover](https://i.ebayimg.com/images/g/wfUAAOSwvtRnnnJw/s-l1600.jpg) and [warranty/imprint page](https://i.ebayimg.com/images/g/3EEAAOSwtYFnnnJx/s-l1600.jpg). Both were visually read: cover says 2003; warranty/imprint page says **© Aug. 2002 Shimano Inc.**, printed in Japan. This is the manual's publication/imprint evidence, not proof that BB-UN73 appears inside it or that the part launched in August 2002.
- [MOMBAT's literature inventory](https://mombatbicycles.com/MOMBAT/PartsForSalePages/literature.html), **LIT170**, lists a **2003 Shimano dealer support catalogue, about 212 pages**, bundled with 2001 support and 2003 XTR/disc-brake documents. Treat this as a separately listed potential physical copy; the exact edition match, continuing availability and BB-UN73 contents have not been verified. The XTR/disc-brake books alone are not substitutes for the general bottom-bracket range. No purchase, enquiry or other contact was made. These paid/holder leads do not establish a free scan.
- Next useful evidence is the actual **BB-UN73 specification or new-product page**, together with the dated cover/imprint, from the 2003 manual or another dated manufacturer source. A full general manual scan or a page supplied by a confirmed holder would justify reopening. Its presence would confirm availability; an explicit new/introduction statement is needed to call the year a maker-confirmed launch. A positive maker withdrawal statement is separately required to establish discontinuation. No page number or unseen product claim has been inferred from the cover.
- Read-only live check: **121** remains **2003–2007**, source_ref **408**, with zero bike links and the existing qualified description: `Sealed square-taper cartridge BB; hollow steel spindle, BC1.37 threads, 68/73mm shells. 110/113mm axles for both shell widths; 122.5mm for 68mm. MY2003 introduction reported; 2007 is latest checked attestation, not discontinuation.` The new lead changes the acquisition action, not those values. No SQL or mapping regeneration was needed. The broad source-lead search TODO is replaced by this concrete page-recovery target; the underlying manufacturer-date question remains unresolved. Task-only thread HTML and the two document photographs were discarded after recording the URLs and limits here.

## CS-1000 variants and FH-RM50 — applied and validated 2026-11-22

Shimano's 1989 Exage dealer manual (source **78**, printed August 1988)
shows CS-1000-6/7 and the seven-speed CN-UG50 pairing on [p. 10](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_-_the_new_system_component_family_for_every_riding_style_page_10_main_image.jpg).
[Page 32](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_-_the_new_system_component_family_for_every_riding_style_page_32_main_image.jpg)
specifies silver steel sprockets and lists 13–23T, 13–26T and 13–28T as the
standard seven-speed combinations. [Service instruction R-45](https://si.shimano.com/en/pdfs/si/R-45/SI-R-45-000-00-ENG.pdf)
prints the separate CS-1000-6 and CS-1000-7 codes. The [1989 Fuji Edition 19
specification table, scan p. 32](https://ebykr.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/ebykr-1989-fuji-catalog-p32.webp)
explicitly lists a silver 13–24T seven-speed CS-1000 on Club Fuji with Sport
LX and A450 hubs (existing source **412**).

- **12237** added `Shimano CS-1000-7, Sport LX` in Cassettes, 1989–1989,
  source 78, stable key `81aa79f9-9dcb-491f-a4f0-b23383217bd2`. It records
  Shimano's standard combinations and Fuji's 13–24T fitment.
- **7564** retains its six-speed identity. The [FH-A450-6 exploded view](https://si.shimano.com/en/pdfs/ev/FH-A450-6-0872/EV-FH-A450-6-0872.pdf)
  explicitly lists silver CS-1000 sprockets. The [1990 dealer manual,
  p. 63](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_-_dealers_1990_product_manual_page_63_main_image.jpg)
  also lists Standard/Black. The description now documents both finishes
  and includes the hubs from R-45.
- **7624** retains the 1989 Exage manual's six-speed specifications. Shimano
  [T-17, May 1989](https://si.shimano.com/en/pdfs/si/T-17/SI-T-17-000-00-ENG.pdf)
  also lists FH-RM50 among seven-speed SIS options. The description records
  that attestation without claiming identical bodies or geometry.
- **1196** now records the [specimen's](https://www.velobase.com/Pages/ViewComponent.aspx?ID=f0c65e0f-8c6a-4209-b74e-ab469abc4a41)
  reported 14–34T and 3.3 mm spacers, with the pictured dark steel sprockets.
  The [FH-6208-5/-6 parts sheet](https://si.shimano.com/de/pdfs/ev/FH-6208-6_5-0734/EV-FH-6208-6_5-0734D.pdf)
  lists a silver 34T sprocket for that family. The black-gear sheets in the
  follow-up below establish further 600EX options. 1196 remains undated and
  separate from 1200.

Bianchi Sport SX has the same gearing, speed count and finish as the Club
Fuji, but names RM-50 hubs and omits the cassette code. T-17 lists both
CS-1000-7 and CS-HG50. Shimano's [1990 Hyperglide table, p. 64](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/00_main_images/shimano_-_dealers_1990_product_manual_page_64_main_image.jpg)
contains no standard 13–24T combination. CS-1000-7 is the leading candidate
and spec 18494 remains NULL pending Bianchi-specific evidence.

Previous descriptions for restoration:

- 7564 `Shimano CS-1000, standard (6sp), Japan, 6-speed freehub cassette, black, 13-16T threaded top, spline 14-34T, for FH-1050 and Deore freehubs`
- 7624 `Shimano FH-RM50, Japan, Aug 1988 Exage manual: 6-speed SIS freehub, one-piece, sealed, QR or nut, 126 / 130mm, 32 / 36h, 446g w/o sprockets, 13-16T / 14-32T; Exage Action, Motion, Trail, Country`
- 1196 `Shimano uni-glide 5 speed, Japan`

Readback verified the three description updates and the new component with
zero bike links. Transaction replay changed zero rows. The three duplicate
reports found no candidates involving 12237. The generator comparison
across 118 loaded CSVs and 24,829 specs and the DB quality findings were
identical before and after.

## Black 14–34T Uniglide spares — 2026-11-22

The [FH-6207-5 parts sheet](https://si.shimano.com/de/pdfs/ev/FH-6207-5-0606/EV-FH-6207-5-0606A.pdf)
dated 16 November 1984 (`8411-U606A`) lists black threaded 14T **357 1410**
and black 34T **357 3432-1** for New 600EX five-speed. Its spacer A/B codes
are **357 5800 / 357 5900**. The [FH-6208 five-/six-speed black-gear sheet](https://si.shimano.com/de/pdfs/ev/FH-6208-0812/EV-FH-6208-0812B.pdf)
with March 1988 print code `8803-E812B` lists black threaded 14T **104 1410**
and black 34T **104 3430**, with spacers **357 5800 / 104 4100**. Both sheets
list the same five-speed fixing bolt **357 6000** and complete freewheel
body **359 9821**. The sheets list individual service sprockets and give
no dimension for spacer A or B.

Compared the [1196 specimen photograph](https://www.velobase.com/CompImages/Cassette/2BD8F44F-41B6-473E-A044-1E54B133866F.jpeg)
with a [loose black 34T sprocket](https://www.vintagecyclebits.com/product/20076301/shimano-early-600-vintage-70-s-34t-freehub-uniglide-w-cut-cassette-cog-nos-bxcc0059-9008-08-20-25).
The specimen shows cutouts but obscures the bore and spacer construction.
Neither photograph provides a readable Shimano service part number. The
14–34T span and finish fit more than one parts sheet. The reported 3.3 mm
spacing remains unverified against a dated Shimano spacer specification.
Seek an original package, a stamped sprocket code or a disassembled view
with a distinguishing feature. No date or provenance update was applied to
1196. The 1978–83 evidence for 1200 describes the earlier sprocket range.

## Cinelli Alivio first-generation fitment — 2026-11-21, read-only

Existing TODO target **20177**, c.1994 Cinelli Velociraptor rear derailleur,
source **378**, prints only `Shimano Alivio` and remains NULL. Live inventory
has neither RD-MC10 nor RD-MC11; later **4475 RD-MC12** (1995–96) and

used or redated.

Visually checked the original Shimano 1994 European catalogue, source **173**,
printed August1993: [p.30 group illustrations](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_94_august_1993_page_30.html)
explicitly introduces two Alivio configurations for1994. The
[p.31 specification table](https://www.disraeligears.co.uk/site/shimano_bicycle_system_components_-_94_august_1993_page_31.html)
distinguishes **RD-MC10's resin plate body** from **RD-MC11's aluminium/barrel-finish
plate body**. Both have aluminium bracket bodies, seven-speed SIS, Advanced
Light Action, 28T maximum /11T minimum sprockets,18T maximum front difference
and35T total capacity. The261g figure explicitly belongs to MC10, not both.
These are distinct constructions, not interchangeable codes to combine in a
single row merely to make the bike link possible.

The original [Cinelli brochure](https://saarf.london/2012/08/22/1982-cinelli-catalogue/)
spread12 gives no model code. Spread20's full Velociraptor photograph leaves
the rear mechanism only a few tens of pixels across in the available904px-wide
scan; it cannot reliably distinguish the plate-body material. The nearby
crank close-up belongs to Argento Vivo, and must not be used to identify the
Velociraptor's equipment. Shared group naming or seven-speed gearing cannot
choose MC10 versus MC11. The Cinelli publication date remains an attributed
c.1994, not a printed imprint.

Result: no component inserts, date changes, SQL or generator changes prepared.
Adding both missing derailleurs alone would not resolve20177. Reopen this
fitment only for an original model code or a sufficiently detailed original
Velociraptor photo showing its plate body. Exact-code/Velociraptor searches
found no such evidence. The existing TODO action was narrowed in place; no
additional inventory/hunt task was added. Downloaded catalogue pages and
inspection crops were removed after recording the source URLs and result.

**New manufacturer evidence:** Shimano's [North American discontinued-products
list](https://bike.shimano.com/en-NA/products/discontinued.html), read2026-11-20,
explicitly lists **BB-UN73 | XT**. Its introductory caveat covers products already
discontinued **or scheduled to be discontinued shortly**. It supplies no
model-level retirement date and does not distinguish those two states per row.
This is positive maker evidence of inclusion in a retirement list and XT
classification, not proof that production ended in2007 or on the observation
date. The initial progress update overstated this as confirmed discontinued
status; the page's full caveat above is the reviewed conclusion. Do not use
today's web observation to extend year_to to2026 or substitute an invented
withdrawal year. This narrows the previous request for a positive withdrawal
statement to a **model-specific dated** manufacturer notice.

**New acquisition lead:** [HoopRider Parts product spc03](https://www.hoopriderparts.com/product/2003-shimano-bicycle-components-trade-salessupport-manual)
lists the2003 Bicycle Components Trade Sales & Support Manual and describes
212 complete pages, full component-group range and compatibility/technical
information. Its public page was readable through the web reader; direct curl
returned403, and no publicly readable BB-UN73 interior page or download was
recovered. Treat it as another physical-copy listing, not a confirmed
BB-UN73-page holder or scan. Availability and exact edition/content would need
rechecking if acquisition were chosen. No purchase/contact was made; paid
acquisition was not proposed as the next executable action. The previously
verified August2002 English(U) imprint and MOMBAT lead remain in the prior notes.

**Specimen lead checked 2026-11-22.** The [Velosaloon listing URL](https://www.velosaloon.com/products/nos-shimano-bb-un73-sealed-cartridge-bottom-bracket-in-115-mm-with-english-thread-from-2002)
attributes the cartridge to 2002. The four photos confirm the BB-UN73
label, 68 mm shell and `BC1.37 × 24`, with `D-H` between the left/right
arrows on the label. Shimano's [2005 specification, ATB/NEXAVE bottom bracket table](https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2005_SPECIFICATION.pdf)
defines `D-H` as the 115 mm axle marking. The photos supply no independently
readable production date or dated box. The 2002 attribution does not extend
the documented range or recover the manual page.

### BB-UN73 availability and axle follow-up — 2026-11-22, applied and validated

An [owner's installation report dated 30 June 2003](https://www.cyclingforums.com/threads/shimano-cartridge-bb-question-16906)
and an [independent August 2003 service discussion](https://www.cyclingforums.com/threads/shimano-vs-capagnolo-vs-truvativ-22318)
attest the cartridge in use in 2003. The [April 2007 technical account](https://veloapocalypse.wordpress.com/articles/phil-wood-y-shimano-bottom-bracket/)
reports a retailer's replacement announcement. Shimano's [MY2007 p. 59](https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2007_SPECIFICATION.pdf#page=59)
is the latest verified maker specification. The range records availability. The
exact maker launch and withdrawal dates remain unverified. The 2003 manual
and any dated withdrawal notice are future source evidence for the notes.

The [68 × 115 mm specimen](https://www.velosaloon.com/products/nos-shimano-bb-un73-sealed-cartridge-bottom-bracket-in-115-mm-with-english-thread-from-2002)
shows BB-UN73, 68 mm, BC1.37 × 24 and D-H on its maker label. Shimano's
[MY2005 specification](https://productinfo.shimano.com/pdfs/product/archive/2005_SPECIFICATION.pdf)
decodes D-H as 115 mm in its chainline tables. The [boxed 68 × 110 mm sample](https://www.velosaloon.com/products/nos-nib-shimano-bb-un73-b10-sealed-cartridge-bottom-bracket-in-110-mm-with-english-thread-from-2003)
visibly carries the MM110 marking and Ultegra/Deore XT packaging. Neither
specimen substantiates an exact year of manufacture.

Applied description for **121** (204 characters):
`Sealed square-taper cartridge, hollow steel spindle, BC1.37 x 24. 110/113 mm axles for 68/73 mm shells, 122.5 mm for 68 mm. A 68 x 115 mm D-H unit is documented. In use by June 2003 and listed for MY2007.`
Exact readback verified the description, **2003–2007**, source **408** and zero
linked bikes. Guarded SQL affected one row, then zero on replay. To restore,
guard by ID, UUID, source, dates and the applied description, then reinstate
the 231-character description recorded in the 2026-11-15 review above.
